# 0. BLUF

The ACMS/A2A production path was previously proven end-to-end. Phase A completed backend hygiene, Phase B completed IDs/handoffs/artifacts/repository metrics/power APIs/search, and Phase C completed the operator UI and final production demonstration.

A new real-world test now fails:

```text
Jira STNA-88
status = TO START
assignee = Startup Teams
human clicks "Check Jira now"
ACMS says reconciliation run completed
BUT:
  no Work Item appears
  no worker executes
  no handoff/artifact appears
  no Jira execution notes appear
  requested ACMS version change does not happen
```

Therefore:

```text
PRIORITY 0:
RESTORE AND PROVE JIRA -> ACMS -> AGENT -> JIRA
```

Only after that proof passes:

```text
PRIORITY 1:
BUILD INTERNAL MCP GATEWAY MVP
```

Approved MCP shape:

```text
Internal AI Agent
      |
      | MCP
      v
https://mcp.miam.home.arpa/
      |
      +-- acms.*
      +-- llm.*
      +-- runtime.*
      +-- proxmox.*
      +-- github.*
      +-- pdu.*

```

The gateway is one logical endpoint, but not one giant root-access service. Domain services keep authority. Existing REST/A2A/SSE stay in place.

# W4.1 - REQUIRED DHCP ISOLATION REMEDIATION

## Human decision

Use a **dedicated ACMS sandbox DHCP class and dedicated address range**.

Selected proposed range:

```text
10.0.20.180 - 10.0.20.189
```

Class name:

```text
ACMS_SANDBOX
```

Why this block:

```text
- documented normal DHCP pool begins at 10.0.20.190
- therefore .180-.189 is outside the currently documented general pool
- known current worker addresses .203 and .206-.209 are not in this block
- known dashboard/service addresses from inventory do not currently occupy this block
```

**This is a proposed reserved block based on the documented inventory, not proof that every address is unused.**

Before changing DHCP, verify all ten addresses live.

Required evidence must include:

```text
Kea leases
OPNsense/Kea reservations
ARP/neighbour table
Proxmox VM/LXC inventory
MIAM Service Registry
internal DNS
ping/ICMP where meaningful
targeted non-invasive service probe where needed
```

If **any** address in `.180-.189` is actually occupied or reserved:

```text
DO NOT USE THE BLOCK
```

Select the next clean contiguous 10-address block that is:

```text
inside 10.0.20.0/24
outside the general DHCP pool
outside all static infrastructure assignments
outside all reservations
outside protected/network infrastructure
```

Document the replacement block in `PLAN-OF-RECORD.md` before enabling it.

## Important: fix the broader static-vs-dynamic collision risk too

A sandbox-specific pool prevents sandbox clients from receiving worker addresses, but the existing general DHCP configuration can still collide with static infrastructure if static addresses sit inside a dynamic pool without protection.

Therefore W4.1 must also inventory **all static addresses inside every Kea dynamic pool**.

At minimum inspect the known worker statics:

```text
10.0.20.203
10.0.20.206
10.0.20.207
10.0.20.208
10.0.20.209
```

Do not re-IP the production workers during this remediation unless absolutely required.

Preferred immediate protection:

```text
explicit Kea reservation/exclusion for each live static infrastructure address
```

Use the method supported by the actual OPNsense/Kea deployment and prove it prevents allocation to unrelated DHCP clients.

## Sandbox identification

Preferred:

```text
DHCP client class / explicit sandbox marker = ACMS_SANDBOX
```

Server Manager must ensure every sandbox is identifiable before first network boot.

Use the cleanest mechanism actually supported by the live DHCP stack, for example:

```text
DHCP user-class / client-class marker
or
Server-Manager-created sandbox MAC reservation inside the sandbox-only pool
```

Do not invent unsupported Kea syntax.

If class-based allocation cannot be implemented safely through the current OPNsense/Kea configuration, use Server Manager managed reservations **within the dedicated sandbox pool** as the fallback, while preserving the same isolation policy.

## Allocation rules

```text
ACMS sandbox:
  may receive only sandbox-pool addresses

ordinary/general DHCP client:
  must never receive sandbox-pool addresses

static infrastructure:
  must never be dynamically allocated to unrelated clients

production workers:
  keep current addresses during this window
```

## Server Manager integration

Before ARM powers on a new sandbox:

```text
1. allocate/identify sandbox network identity
2. ensure it maps to ACMS_SANDBOX
3. confirm an available sandbox-pool lease exists
4. verify selected IP is not already active/reserved
5. persist Work UID + Agent UID + VMID + MAC + lease/IP relationship
6. only then boot
```

If no sandbox address is available:

```text
FAIL CLOSED
do not borrow from the general DHCP pool
do not boot with an unclassified DHCP identity
```

## Lease visibility

Expose enough state for debugging:

```text
sandbox VMID
Work UID
Agent UID
MAC
DHCP class
leased IP
lease start
lease expiry
Kea lease state
```

Prefer surfacing this through Server Manager and later MCP/runtime resources.

## W4.1 acceptance test

Create at least **two disposable sandboxes** through the real production path.

Prove:

```text
both receive unique sandbox-pool addresses
neither receives .203 or .206-.209
neither receives any non-sandbox infrastructure static
general DHCP client cannot consume sandbox-only address
sandbox cannot fall back into general pool
worker-001 stays healthy throughout
no duplicate ARP/MAC/IP condition
no duplicate Kea lease
TTL/extend/sweep still work
destroy_own returns lease cleanly
next sandbox can reuse released pool capacity safely
```

Then run a concurrency test:

```text
create >=2 sandboxes near-simultaneously
```

Prove no duplicate allocation.

After acceptance:

```text
sandbox create may be UNPAUSED
```

## DHCP rollback

Before mutation, export/capture:

```text
OPNsense DHCP/Kea config
active lease table
reservations
pool configuration
relevant class configuration
```

Rollback if:

```text
normal LAN DHCP fails
existing leases are unexpectedly invalidated
sandbox gets an address outside sandbox pool
ordinary client gets sandbox address
static infrastructure becomes unreachable
Kea fails to reload
```

Rollback must restore the previous DHCP config and keep sandbox creation paused.

## DHCP stop conditions

STOP if:

```text
.180-.189 occupancy cannot be determined confidently
OPNsense/Kea cannot express safe isolation without disrupting current clients
a proposed change would renumber existing production workers
DHCP reload would disconnect critical infrastructure
sandbox classification is not deterministic before first network boot
```

Do not work around DHCP isolation by hard-coding random IPs in templates.

Do NOT\n  reintroduce host-side `127.0.0.1:8000` probes — the app publishes no host\n  ports (nginx is the sole ingress by design). Live-proven 12/12 exit 0 after\n  the 137a008 deploy. **Also: never ship a check-script that counts failures\n  but always exits 0 — smoke-test.sh had exactly that bug (FAILURES counted,\n  no exit 1); scripts that gate anything must propagate failure to the exit\n  code, and `exit 0` printed after a pipeline (`cmd | tail`) is `tail`'s\n  status, not the script's — use `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`.**\n- **Pipeline status (2026-09-26): fully routine.** Drill 3 = \"Release ACCEPTED\"\n  end-to-end (preflight → verified backup → maintenance → build → migrate →\n  ready 2s → 6/6 + 12/12 validation, exit 0); routine deploys #137a008 and\n  #9545123 (v0.5.0) each ACCEPTED with zero intervention, smoke 12/12.\n  **Standing deploy cadence: user message \"PR N merged\" → fetch new main tip →\n  `bash deploy/release.sh <tip-sha>` on CT122 → report ACCEPTED + smoke.** No\n  manual hop unless the on-box tree is somehow behind the transaction baseline\n  (bootstrap-hop rule applies only to first-ever tooling install).\n- **Slice-2 view delivered (9545123, v0.5.0):** Agent Detail page at\n  `/ui/agents/{agent_id}` — read-only; identity + capability manifest\n  (declared vs not-declared flags rendered honestly), assignment/execution-\n  task/handoff history, background-routine inventory (REQ-014) with explicit\n  \"ACMS does not schedule\" note, recorded-only delivery notice (shared\n  `DELIVERY_STATE` constant from work_routes), and an explicit\n  heartbeat-pending note (slice 3) — never fabricate liveness. Home \"not yet implemented\"\n  list must track what the backend actually maintains.\n- **Tooling-self-replacement caveat:** when a target tip CHANGES\n  `deploy/release.sh` (or rollback/validate scripts), the running transaction\n  checks out the new tree mid-flight while bash still reads the old script\n  from disk — unproven territory. Never put secrets in release metadata.\n- Feature-specific smoke hooks via `ACMS_FEATURE_SMOKE` env (plan §11).\n\n## Live CT122 quirks (validated 2026-09-26)\n\n- Git checkout is at `/opt/acms/repo` (NOT `/opt/acms`); `.env` at\n  `/opt/acms/.env` (0600, key names only — never echo values).\n- **Compose v2 container names are `acms-<service>-1`** — bare\n  `docker exec acms-app` FAILS (\"No such container\"). Always\n  `docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env exec -T\n  <service>` (service-name agnostic). Never re-introduce a single-file conf mount.\n- **`docker compose up -d <svc>` recreates depends_on services too** and can\n  bring the app back with the default image (`acms-app:local`). Echoes can mislead in BOTH directions (phantom\nerrors and phantom success; this session had mid-write corruption injecting\ngarbage text). For large/critical writes: verify immediately after writing,\nand if corrupted, repair via a python rewrite with assertion pre-checks on\nknown anchor lines, then re-verify with git diff.\n\n## PR-discipline trap (hit TWICE on 2026-09-29 — mandatory habit)\n\n`gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch` checks the LOCAL checkout out onto\n`main` when it deletes the branch. A following `git push -q` (or any commit +\npush) then goes DIRECT to main — Jordan's token has admin rights, so branch\nprotection silently bypasses (remote prints \"Bypassed rule violations\" but the\npush lands). Happened twice in one window (c2d68d7, 8575982; content was\ngreen, the process was violated). **Habit: after every merge-with-delete-branch,\nrun `git branch --show-current` BEFORE any commit; if on main, `git switch -c\n<branch> origin/main` first.** Never `git push` bare from a merge aftermath.\n\n**PERMANENT FIXES now installed on the agent workstation (window-4, plan §D;\nstill keep the habit — the guard is local to this machine):**\n- `~/.config/git/hooks/pre-push` via global `core.hooksPath` — REFUSES\n  pushes to `main|master|production` before the network is touched\n  (behavior-tested in a scratch repo). Retries 0/10/30/60s on transient failure; permanent\n  rejections (403/404/503) stop immediately; watch lifetime bounded (2h\n  default, RUN_WATCH_EXPIRED audit); reconcile remains fallback. Settings:\n  ACMS_CALLBACK_WATCH_ENABLED (default true), ACMS_CALLBACK_SELF_URL (default\n  in-container loopback :8000 — nginx 403s compose-network sources),\n  ACMS_CALLBACK_WATCH_POLL_SECONDS/MAX_SECONDS. **CRITICAL pitfall: the\n  watcher is an asyncio task in the APP process — dispatch MUST go through\n  the API route (`POST /api/v1/dispatch/work/{id}`). Creds `~/.pdu_lldap_service_creds` (0600).\n- In-VM shell: SSH as `jordatech` (10.0.20.156) + passwordless sudo — the reliable path (qga is inactive on VM156; the PVE-API guest-exec path works for VM154 when it is booted).\n- TLS cert: self-signed, DER sha256\n  `137308d592260184fd75b7a555e27f61332a8c7ffe5feb1f58d1c5553b2221a9`.\n\n## Architecture (what runs where)\n- systemd `pdu-control`: gunicorn 1 worker × 4 threads, **127.0.0.1:5000**\n  ONLY. nginx terminates TLS :443 (config\n  /etc/nginx/sites-available/pdu-control). nftables `inet pdu_filter` allows\n  80/443/22 from 10.0.20.0/24 + 10.0.10.0/24, drops 80/443 otherwise.\n- Modules: `app.py` (UI + legacy /api routes) → `action_service.py` (single\n  action path + invariants + idempotency) → `app_runtime.py` (locks, audit,\n  worker subprocess via `pdu_worker.py` → `pdu_ssh_direct.py` pexpect\n  PowerAlert SSH driver — DO NOT touch the driver).\n- `auth_lldap.py`: bind-check + `(member=<userDN>)` group search via service\n  bind `svc-pdu-manager-vm154`; 60s group cache (revocation lands ≤60s).\n\n## LLDAP facts (10.0.20.101)\n- LDAP protocol port **3890** (NOT 17170 — that's web/GraphQL UI).\n- Base DN `dc=example,dc=com`; user DN `uid=<user>,ou=people,...`.\n- NO memberOf: search `ou=groups,dc=example,dc=com` w/ `(member=<userDN>)`.\n- No GraphQL password mutation: use ldap3 ModifyPassword extop bound as admin.\n- Groups: pdu-viewer(11) pdu-operator(12) pdu-admin(13) pdu-ai-agent(14)\n  pdu-ai-admin-override(15).\n\n## Hard invariants (never weaken)\n- Protected outlets (153: [3,4,5,6,9]): OFF forbidden for EVERYONE\n  (PROTECTED_OFF_FORBIDDEN), even admin_override/root. Override = REBOOT only,\n  requiring admin_override + reason + acknowledge_protected_device (+ for\n  153/9: acknowledge_controller_may_go_offline). Expected final state ON.\n- Protected reboot = native PDU `4- Cycle Load` (atomic, PDU-committed). Never\n  implement OFF-then-ON in the controller.\n- 153/9 reboot: writes `/var/lib/pdu-control/pending_reboot.json`, commits the\n  cycle, VM dies mid-verify, boot reconciler verifies/recovers ON + audits.\n\n## API usage pattern (agents)\nGET /me → GET outlet → one UUID Idempotency-Key per intended action → POST\naction → poll GET /jobs/{id} → verify state. Never retry with a NEW request id\non timeout (idempotency replays the original job; new id = new power event).\nRate limits: 120 GET/min, 20 POST/min per identity (429 + Retry-After).\nIdempotency persists across restarts (/var/lib/pdu-control/idempotency.json).\n\n## Pitfalls learned\n- secrets.env must stay 640 root:pducontrol (chmod 600 breaks the service).\n- gunicorn must stay --workers 1 (runtime structures are per-process).\n- Dashboard cold-load does 3 serial PDU SSH state reads (~30-60s) — normal.\n- Config JSON at /etc/pdu-control/config.json is the protection source of\n  truth; backup dir /root/pdu-control-backups/20260910-222303/.\n- Rollback: `qm rollback 154 pre_lldap_api_upgrade_20260910` on miam-00133.\n- Deploy via guest-exec needs ≤12KB base64 chunks per write (596 broken pipe\n  on big single posts).\n- LLDAP GraphQL: `user(userId:)` not `user(id:)`; introspect mutations.\n\n## Asset-addressed API (REV4 §10C Phases 2-6, LIVE since 2026-09-27)\n\n- **Asset mapping = Git-managed labels.** Each outlet's label carries `MIAM-##### - <description>`; the FIRST `MIAM-#####` token is the outlet's asset_id (⚠️ labels use 5-digit numbers — a `MIAM-\\d{3}` regex silently matches a 3-digit prefix of `MIAM-00167` and creates phantom duplicates). `build_asset_index()` in `app/api_assets.py` derives the mapping and raises `ASSET_MAPPING_AMBIGUOUS` (500) if one asset_id appears on two outlets.\n- **Raw-route identity guard (Phase 3):** raw outlet actions accept `expected_asset_id` in the JSON body; mismatch → `409 TARGET_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` BEFORE any driver dispatch. Live-verified: POST with expected=MIAM-00119 against PDU asset MIAM-00151 was refused pre-dispatch. Machine callers SHOULD send it; the wrong-target incident is why this exists.\n- **Dry-run plans (Phase 4):** `POST /api/v1/action-plans` returns `actuated: false` + target resolution + current state + protected policy + caller authz + required acks. Use it to pre-verify identity before a real submit. `GET /capabilities` + `GET /version` expose API version, backend mode, asset count, supported actions, rate limits.\n- **Correlation metadata (Phase 6):** every action accepts `correlation_id` / `source_service` / `upstream_operation_id`; they land in both the human audit line and a structured JSONL audit record (`action_submitted` event). Feed them from ACMS Work / Server Manager ops.\n- **Reusable client (Phase 8):** `pdu_manager_client.py` in the repo root — TLS-verify required by default (verify=False is an explicit opt-out only), NO auto-minted idempotency keys after timeout, structured exceptions (`PduIdentityMismatch`, `PduProtectedPolicy`, `PduAuth`), correlation passthrough, `wait_for_job`. `tests/test_pdu_client.py` covers the contract.\n- **Import-time backend selection:** `app_runtime.py` reads `PDU_BACKEND` at import. Test files MUST set `PDU_BACKEND=mock` in conftest BEFORE importing app modules (a per-test monkeypatch is too late if another test imported app first) — this is the REQ-010 structural \"CI never actuates hardware\" guarantee.\n## Live power-cycle test protocol (learned 2026-09-27, authorized 00119 test)\n\n- **Verified mapping:** `151:9 = MIAM-00119` (PVE node, Dell 7010). OFF dropped the node (~10s), ON restored it, PDU REBOOT (native Cycle Load) cleanly rebooted it. # AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

### [2026-10-02T17:09:27.710496+00:00] assistant

Found a **live-found W5 gap**: the CLI `cmd_mint_agent` hardcodes `scopes=["acms.read","acms.write"]`, overriding the store's full-domain defaults — so agent tokens minted via CLI lack `power.read`/`proxmox.write` (same class as the W2 scope gap, PR #80). The internal auto-mint path (dispatch envelope) uses the store defaults, so workers are fine; only CLI mints are affected.

This is exactly the plan's §W4.1-style acceptance defect class. Fix: CLI passes `scopes=None` to inherit store defaults. Also checking `grant-scopes` exists for the probe token. I'll: (1) fix CLI, (2) run tests, (3) PR + merge (session auto-merge steer active), (4) deploy, (5) re-mint probe, (6) run live probes.

{"output": "def add_comment(self, issue_key: str, body_markdown: str) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Post a useful summary comment (status summaries, handoffs, links).\n\n        Body is converted to ADF (Jira REST v3 requires a document body — a raw\n        string 400s with 'Comment body is not valid!'); markdown structure\n        (headings/bullets/code) is preserved where mappable.\n        \"\"\"\n        self._check_projects(issue_key)\n        if self.is_mock:\n            self._record(\"add_comment\", key=issue_key, body=body_markdown)\n            return f\"mock-comment-{len(self.mock_calls)}\"\n        with httpx.Client(base_url=self.base_url, auth=self._auth(), timeout=20) as c:\n            r = c.post(f\"/rest/api/3/issue/{issue_key}/comment\",\n                       json={\"body\": JiraClient._markdown_to_adf(body_markdown)})\n            r.raise_for_status()\n            return r.json().get(\"id\", \"\")\n\n    def transition_status(self, issue_key: str, target_status: str) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Config-flagged OFF by default (plan §D6). Mock records the attempt.\"\"\"\n        if not self.status_mutation_enabled:\n            raise JiraError(\n                \"Jira status mutation is disabled (ACMS_JIRA_STATUS_MUTATION_ENABLED=false); \"\n                \"v1 posts comments/summaries only\"\n            )\n        self._check_projects(issue_key)\n        if self.is_mock:\n            self._record(\"transition_status\", key=issue_key, target=target_status)\n            return True\n        with httpx.Client(base_url=self.base_url, auth=self._auth(), timeout=20) as c:\n            r = c.get(f\"/rest/api/3/issue/{issue_key}/transitions\")\n            r.raise_for_status()\n            match = next((t for t in r.json().get(\"transitions\", [])\n                          if t.get(\"name\", \"\").lower() == target_status.lower()), None)\n            if match is None:\n                raise JiraError(f\"transition '{target_status}' not available for {issue_key}\")\n            r2 = c.post(f\"/rest/api/3/issue/{issue_key}/transitions/{match['id']}\")\n            r2.raise_for_status()\n            return True", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "def find_work_item(self, ref: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n        \"\"\"Resolve a Work reference: UID, short key, or UUID. None when absent.\"\"\"\n        if not ref:\n            return None\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", f\"/api/v1/work/items/{ref}\")\n        if status == 200:\n            return body\n        # list + filter by UID/key (the detail route may accept only UUIDs)\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", \"/api/v1/work/items\")\n        if status != 200 or not isinstance(body, list):\n            raise DomainUnavailableError(f\"ACMS work list failed (HTTP {status})\")\n        for item in body:\n            if ref in (item.get(\"work_item_id\"), item.get(\"work_key\"), item.get(\"work_uid\")):\n                return item\n        return None\n\n    def active_assignment(self, agent_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", \"/api/v1/work/assignments\", query={\"agent_id\": agent_id, \"status\": \"ACTIVE\"})\n        if status != 200 or not isinstance(body, list):\n            raise DomainUnavailableError(f\"ACMS assignments failed (HTTP {status})\")\n        return body[0] if body else None\n\n    def get_project(self, ref: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", f\"/projects/{ref}\")\n        if status == 200:\n            return body\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", \"/projects\")\n        if status != 200 or not isinstance(body, list):\n            raise DomainUnavailableError(f\"ACMS projects failed (HTTP {status})\")\n        for p in body:\n            if ref in (p.get(\"project_id\"), p.get(\"slug\"), p.get(\"name\")):\n                return p\n        return None\n\n    def get_product(self, product_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", f\"/products/{product_id}\")\n        if status == 200:\n            return body\n        return None\n\n    def find_artifact(self, ref: str, *, work_item_id: str | None = None) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", f\"/artifacts/{ref}\")\n        if status == 200:\n            return body\n        # artifact detail route accepts UUID/UID/sha; fall back to list filter\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", \"/artifacts\")\n        if status != 200 or not isinstance(body, list):\n            raise DomainUnavailableError(f\"ACMS artifacts failed (HTTP {status})\")\n        for a in body:\n            if ref in (a.get(\"artifact_id\"), a.get(\"artifact_uid\")):\n                return a\n        return None\n\n    def artifact_content(self, artifact_id: str) -> str | None:\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", f\"/artifacts/{artifact_id}/content\")\n        if status != 200:\n            return None\n        if isinstance(body, dict):\n            return body.get(\"content\")\n        return body if isinstance(body, str) else None\n\n    def get_agent(self, agent_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", f\"/api/v1/agents/{agent_id}\")\n        if status == 200:\n            return body\n        status, body = self.request(\"GET\", \"/api/v1/agents\")\n        if status != 200 or not isinstance(body, list):\n            raise DomainUnavailableError(f\"ACMS agents failed (HTTP {status})\")\n        for a in body:\n            if ref_match(agent_id, a):\n                return a\n        return None\n\n    # ---------------- scope enforcement helper ----------------\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def assert_assignment_scope(identity: CallIdentity, work: dict[str, Any],\n                                assignment: dict[str, Any] | None) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Raise OutOfScopeError unless identity may act on this work item.\"\"\"\n        if identity.has_role(\"executive\", \"infrastructure_admin\"):\n            return\n        if identity.is_assignment_scoped:\n            if identity.work_uid and work.get(\"work_uid\") != identity.work_uid                and work.get(\"work_key\") != identity.work_uid                and work.get(\"work_item_id\") != identity.work_uid:\n                raise OutOfScopeError(\n                    f\"assignment token is bound to {identity.work_uid}; \"\n                    f\"{work.get('work_uid') or work.get('work_item_id')} is out of scope\"\n                )\n            return\n        # {"output": "\"\"\"Jira kickoff eligibility gate (window-5 plan §7/§13).\n\nThe human-facing planning layer is Jira. A Work Item may be dispatched to AI\nexecution ONLY when:\n\n  1. it carries a Jira linkage (site + immutable issue id + key), and\n  2. the linked issue's CURRENT observed status is a configured READY state\n     (default ``TO START`` — project workflow metadata is verified at runtime,\n     never assumed globally valid), and\n  3. the issue is CURRENTLY assigned to the dedicated AI account\n     (startupteamscompany@gmail.com — resolved to a Jira accountId, matched by\n     accountId, never by display name or email visibility), and\n  4. no local hold (pause/stop) or operator restart authorization is pending.\n\nEligibility is NECESSARY but never sufficient: budgets, runtime health, and\nlocal holds still apply after this gate (dispatch order: Jira → budget → …).\n\nServer-side enforcement: dispatch_service calls :func:`gate_work_item` on every\npath (UI, API, scheduler, retry, resume, Executive decomposition, product\nbootstrap). Drafts may be saved unlinked; they are visibly non-executable.\n\nDesign notes:\n- The gate NEVER mutates Jira. Observation is read-only; transitions are the\n  reconciliation service's concern.\n- Local holds are durable DB rows — a process restart cannot clear them and a\n  poll cannot override them (plan §13.3).\n- One Jira issue may authorize multiple ACMS Work Items (§7.3) — descendants\n  inherit the authorization; the gate checks the TOP-LEVEL linkage up the\n  parent chain as well as the item's own linkage.\n- Observation staleness: eligibility is verified at dispatch time with a LIVE\n  Jira read when credentials are configured (bounded timeout). If Jira is\n  unreachable the gate returns an UNKNOWN verdict — new dispatch is inhibited\n  (fail-closed), and running work keeps going (§13.2 last row).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport time\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import select\nfrom sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\n\nfrom .jira_models import new_id\nfrom .settings import get_settings\nfrom .work_models import WorkItemRecord\n\n# --- verdict vocabulary (persisted; the UI renders these verbatim) -----------\nELIGIBLE = \"ELIGIBLE\"\nNOT_LINKED = \"NOT_LINKED\"\nNOT_READY = \"NOT_READY\"\nASSIGNEE_MISMATCH = \"ASSIGNEE_MISMATCH\"\nLOCAL_HOLD = \"LOCAL_HOLD\"\nUNKNOWN = \"JIRA_UNKNOWN\"\nSTALE_AUTHORIZATION = \"STALE_AUTHORIZATION\"\n\n# Local hold kinds (plan §13.3: pause/stop are persistent local controls)\nHOLD_PAUSE = \"PAUSE\"\nHOLD_STOP = \"STOP\"", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

### [2026-10-02T17:31:55.877362+00:00] assistant

Now I have the full picture. Designing W6 (plan §28):

**Resources (READ):** `jira.issue.get`, `jira.comments.list`, `jira.status.get` — via the ACMS Jira REST credentials (gateway holds its own env-injected Jira token, same contract as ACMS's `ACMS_JIRA_*`).

**Tools (SAFE_WRITE):** `jira.comment.add`, `jira.description.update`, `jira.transition.request` — all transitions ride ACMS policy:
- Worker scope enforcement: the issue key must match the caller's own assignment's `jira_issue_key` (assignment token binding or ACTIVE assignment linkage)
- Transition policy: worker allowed `TO START→IN PROGRESS`, `IN PROGRESS→IN REVIEW` ONLY; BLOCKED → denied for workers (executive/infrastructure_admin may; ordinary worker keeps IN PROGRESS + comment + Inbox raise)
- `jira.template.clone_request` — SAFE_WRITE for authorized clones only
- `ACMS_JIRA_STATUS_MUTATION_ENABLED=false` in prod → transitions fail-closed at the adapter with the same message as ACMS (honest, no bypass)

{"output": "# MIAM Service Registry → Homarr propagation — verified recipe (2026-10-01)\n\nChanging a human-service URL (e.g. ACMS `https://10.0.20.122` → `.../ui`) is a\nTWO-hop change with verification at both ends. Executed live for the ACMS\nrecord during the A2A production-path session.\n\n## Access path (no direct SSH to VM119 needed)\n\nRun curls INSIDE VM119 via qga guest exec (miam-00135, VM119 — qga works on\nthat node):\n\n```bash\nTOKEN=$(grep \"^MIAM_SERVICE_REGISTRY_TOKEN=\" /etc/miam-service-registry/secrets.env | cut -d= -f2-)\nBASE=\"https://registry.miam.home.arpa\"\nRESOLVE=\"--resolve registry.miam.home.arpa:443:127.0.0.1\"   # Caddy vhost on loopback\n```\n\n## Hop 1 — Registry record (FULL-record PATCH only)\n\n1. `GET $BASE/api/v1/services` → find the record (e.g. id `acms`).\n2. **PATCH with the COMPLETE record** — PATCH REPLACES the whole record;\n   omitting `category`/`host`/`tags` ERASES them (documented 09-30, respected\n   10-01). Reconstruct the full JSON from the GET, change ONLY the url.\n3. Re-GET to verify the new url + fresh `updated` timestamp.\n\n## Hop 2 — Homarr card (reconciler propagates within its ~60 s cycle)\n\nThe reconciler (`miam-service-reconciler.service`) syncs Registry → Homarr\nboard automatically; Homarr ownership marker =\n`[MIAM-REGISTRY-ID:<id>]` in the app description.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "`[MIAM-REGISTRY-ID:<id>]` in the app description.\n\nWait one cycle (~70 s), then verify via the Homarr API (ApiKey header — NOT\nx-api-key, which 401s):\n\n```bash\nKEY=$(grep \"^HOMARR_API_KEY=\" /etc/miam-service-registry/secrets.env | cut -d= -f2-)\ncurl -sk -H \"ApiKey: $KEY\" http://127.0.0.1:7575/api/apps | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nfor a in json.load(sys.stdin):\n    if 'acms' in json.dumps(a).lower():\n        print(a.get('name'), '->', a.get('href'), '| ping:', a.get('pingUrl'))\"\n```\n\nExpected: `href` == the new Registry url. If stale: check\n`/var/lib/miam-reconciler/audit.jsonl` and the reconciler service status\nbefore hand-editing anything (never hand-edit Homarr-only state).\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- `health_url` stays the API health endpoint (`.../health`) even when `url`\n  becomes a UI path — Kuma monitors the health URL, humans click the UI URL.\n- Reference: `references/miam-service-registry.md` in\n  `devops/proxmox-cluster-infrastructure` (API surface, token locations,\n  states, the PATCH-replaces-record rule).", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "# MIAM Service Registry + dashboard stack (VM119) — API & operations reference\n\nVerified live 2026-09-30 during the full cluster inventory run.\n\n## Topology (VM119 `miam-service-dashboard`, 10.0.20.172, node miam-00135, onboot=1)\n\n- Docker compose at `/opt/miam-dashboard/compose.yaml`: `caddy:2` (80/443), `homarr`\n  (127.0.0.1:7575), `uptime-kuma` (3001). The registry + reconciler are SYSTEMD services\n  (not containers): `miam-service-registry.service` (uvicorn app:app on 127.0.0.1:8720) and\n  `miam-service-reconciler.service` (60s loop, `/opt/miam-dashboard/reconciler/reconcile.py`).\n- Caddy vhost map: `services.miam.home.arpa` → 7575 (Homarr), `status.` → 3001 (Kuma),\n  `registry.` → 8720. `@allowed remote_ip 10.0.10.0/24 10.0.20.0/24 127.0.0.1 100.64.0.0/10`\n  (CGNAT range added 09-30 for Tailscale clients — subnet-routed traffic keeps its 100.x source).\n- TLS: Caddy internal CA (ECC, ~6h auto-rotated certs). Root CA exportable from\n  `/opt/miam-dashboard/caddy/data/caddy/pki/authorities/local/root.crt`.\n- Dashboard docs live on the VM: `/opt/miam-dashboard/REGISTERING-SERVICES.md` (the mandatory\n  agent rule) and `MARION-IA-USA-SERVICE-DASHBOARD-HANDOFF.md`.\n\n## Registry API (`https://registry.miam.home.arpa`)\n\n- Auth: `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Token files in\n  `/etc/miam-service-registry/tokens/<name>.json` store ONLY\n  `{name, scopes, token_sha256}`. The RAW agent-registrar token lives in\n  `/etc/miam-service-registry/secrets.env` as `MIAM_SERVICE_REGISTRY_TOKEN` (0600).\n  Read it server-side; never print or copy it out.\n- Endpoints (openapi at `/openapi.json`, swagger at `/docs`):\n  `GET /api/v1/services`, `POST /api/v1/services`, `GET|PATCH|PUT /api/v1/services/{sid}`,\n  `POST /api/v1/services/{sid}/disable`, `POST .../enable`, `GET /api/v1/discoveries`, `GET /health`.\n- POST with duplicate id → `{\"detail\":\"id already exists\"}` (safe, non-destructive).\n- ⚠ **PATCH REPLACES THE ENTIRE RECORD** — a PATCH that omits `category`/`host`/`tags` ERASES\n  them (hit live 2026-09-30, restored on a second PATCH). Always PATCH the complete desired record.\n- No DELETE: use `/{sid}/disable` (reversible via `/enable`).\n- From the LAN use `curl --resolve registry.miam.home.arpa:443:10.0.20.172`; from inside VM119,\n  `--resolve ...:127.0.0.1` against https works (Caddy on loopback-bound vhosts).\n- Registrar token scopes: `services:read`, `services:register`, `services:update`.\n- States: `managed | discovered | candidate | disabled | stale`.\n\n## Reconciler behavior (`reconcile.py`)\n\n- Reads `/etc/miam-service-registry/secrets.env` for `HOMARR_API_KEY`, `KUMA_URL/USER/PASS`,\n  `MIAM_SERVICE_REGISTRY_TOKEN`. Loop ~60s; audit at `/var/lib/miam-reconciler/audit.jsonl`.\n- **Kuma sync:** for every NON-disabled registry service, ensure an HTTP monitor named\n  `<service name>` with `url = url || health_url` (socket.io `monitor/add` / `monitor/edit`,\n  interval 60s). Verified live: registering 16 managed records flipped monitors 11 → 19 → 34\n  across cycles. Disabled services are skipped (not monitored).\n  **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-30:** this claim was FALSE — Kuma held 0 monitors (see the Homarr-sync note\n  above and `references/homarr-kuma-api-protocols.md`). The working kuma_sync.py also: converts\n  bearer-gated `:8765` endpoints to `type:\"port\"` TCP monitors (401 ≠ down), needs `conditions: []`\n  in payloads, and holds a flock lockfile to prevent duplicate monitors from concurrent runs.\n- **Homarr sync:** SKIPPED with log `homarr: no API key configured yet — sync skipped` until\n  `HOMARR_API_KEY` exists. Homarr v1.x API keys are created ONLY in the UI (Settings → API Keys);\n  with LDAP-only auth and zero local users there is NO headless path. Human step: create key →\n  `echo \"HOMARR_API_KEY=<key>\" >> /etc/miam-service-registry/secrets.env` → picked up next cycle.\n  (Instruction file staged 09-30: `/etc/miam-service-registry/HOMARR-API-KEY-SETUP.md`.)\n  **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-30 (later same day):** the key is now installed AND this file's sync-behavior claims\n  were WRONG: the original inline `sync_homarr`/`sync_kuma` never worked (wrong Homarr header `x-api-key`\n  → 401; tuple-shape Kuma login → silent no-ack) and their success log counted inputs, not outcomes\n  (\"ensured (34)\" while Kuma held 0 monitors — the \"34 monitors\" claim in the morning handoff was false).\n  Both blocks are now delegated to verified implementations `homarr_sync.py` / `kuma_sync.py` /\n  `kuma_status_page.py` in the same reconciler directory — see `references/homarr-kuma-api-protocols.md`", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Usage: pve_api.py <method> <path> [json_body]\nSpecial commands: login, permissions, realms, nodes, resources,\nvmconfig <node> <vmid>, vmstatus <node> <vmid>, raw <METHOD> <path> [json]\"\"\"\nimport sys, json, ssl, urllib.request, urllib.parse, os\n\nBASE = \"https://10.0.20.100:8006\"\nCTX = ssl.create_default_context()\nCTX.check_hostname = False\nCTX.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\nCREDS = os.path.expanduser(\"~/.pve_ldap_bot\")\n\n\ndef load_creds():\n    with open(CREDS) as f:\n        lines = [l.rstrip(\"\\n\") for l in f if l.strip()]\n    return lines[0], lines[1]\n\n\ndef login():\n    user, pw = load_creds()\n    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({\"username\": user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\", \"password\": pw}).encode()\n    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + \"/api2/json/access/ticket\", data=data)\n    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=10) as r:\n        d = json.load(r)[\"data\"]\n    return d[\"ticket\"], d[\"CSRFPreventionToken\"], user + \"@LLDAP-Domain\"\n\n\ndef api(method, path, body=None):\n    ticket, csrf, _ = login()\n    return raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body)\n\n\ndef raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body=None):\n    data = None\n    if isinstance(body, dict):\n        data = urllib.parse.urlencode(body, doseq=True).encode()\n    elif isinstance(body, str):\n        data = body.encode()", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}
